}\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const DeviceBodies& d, const std::vector<Body>& bodies, double* dPartial) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    if (n == 0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n\n terms.push_back(0.5 * (body.vel.x * body.vel.x +\n                              body.vel.y * body.vel.y +\n                              body.vel.z * body.vel.z));\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy body state\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        // Check for extreme values (bodies shouldn't fly off to infinity)\n        const double maxPos = 1e6;\n        const double maxVel = bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(body.vel.x) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.y) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.z) > maxVel) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of bodies (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -s <num>     Number of simulation steps (default: the j-loop is walked in ascending order in tiles that\n// are staged through shared memory, which keeps the accumulation order (and\n// therefore the floating-point result) identical to the serial version.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_SIZE) void computeForcesKernel(const double* __restrict__ px,\n                                                                  const double* __restrict__ py,\n                                                                  const double* __restrict__ pz, double* __restrict__ vx,\n                                                                  double* __restrict__ vy, double* __restrict__ vz,\n                                                                  const int n) {\n    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);\n    const int warp = threadIdx.x / WARP_SIZE;\n\n    // Bodies owned by this thread: BODIES_PER_THREAD indices, strided by the\n    // warp width so that every global access stays coalesced.\n    const int base = (blockIdx.x * (BLOCK_SIZE / WARP_SIZE) + warp) * (WARP_SIZE * BODIES_PER_THREAD);\n\n    double xi[BODIES_PER_THREAD], yi[BODIES_PER_THREAD], zi[BODIES_PER_THREAD];\n    double Fx[BODIES_PER_THREAD], Fy[BODIES_PER_THREAD], Fz[BODIES_PER_THREAD];\n\n#pragma unroll\n    for (int u = 0; u < BODIES_PER_THREAD; ++u) {\n                const double dx = xj - xi[u];\n                const double dy = yj - yi[u];\n                const double dz = zj - zi[u];\n                const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n                const double invDist = 1.0 / sqrt(distSqr);\n                const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n                Fx[u] += dx * invDist3;\n                Fy[u] += dy * invDist3;\n                Fz[u] += dz * invDist3;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n#pragma unroll\n    for (int u = 0; }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const DeviceBodies& d, const std::vector<Body>& bodies, double* dPartial) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    if (n == 0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n\n terms.push_back(0.5 * (body.vel.x * body.vel.x +\n                              body.vel.y * body.vel.y +\n                              body.vel.z * body.vel.z));\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(body.vel.x) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.y) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.z) > maxVel) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s the j-loop is walked in ascending order in tiles that\n// are staged through shared memory, which keeps the accumulation order (and\n// therefore the floating-point result) identical to the serial version.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_SIZE) void computeForcesKernel(const double* __restrict__ px,\n                                                                  const double* __restrict__ py,\n                                                                  const double* __restrict__ pz, double* __restrict__ vx,\n                                                                  double* __restrict__ vy, double* __restrict__ vz,\n                                                                  const int n) {\n    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);\n    const int warp = threadIdx.x / WARP_SIZE;\n\n    // Bodies owned by this thread: BODIES_PER_THREAD indices, strided by the\n    // warp width so that every global access stays coalesced.\n    const int base = (blockIdx.x * (BLOCK_SIZE / WARP_SIZE) + warp) * (WARP_SIZE * BODIES_PER_THREAD);\n\n    double xi[BODIES_PER_THREAD], yi[BODIES_PER_THREAD], zi[BODIES_PER_THREAD];\n    double Fx[BODIES_PER_THREAD], Fy[BODIES_PER_THREAD], Fz[BODIES_PER_THREAD];\n\n#pragma unroll\n    for (int u = 0; u < BODIES_PER_THREAD; ++u) {\n                const double dx = xj - xi[u];\n                const double dy = yj - yi[u];\n                const double dz = zj - zi[u];\n                const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n                const double invDist = 1.0 / sqrt(distSqr);\n                const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n                Fx[u] += dx * invDist3;\n                Fy[u] += dy * invDist3;\n                Fz[u] += dz * invDist3;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n#pragma unroll\n    for (int u = 0; }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const DeviceBodies& d, const std::vector<Body>& bodies, double* dPartial) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    if (n == 0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n\n Register blocking lets one broadcast j-position\n// feed several i-accumulators, which raises arithmetic intensity and ILP.\n#ifndef BODIES_PER_THREAD\n#define BODIES_PER_THREAD 1\n#endif\n\nconstexpr int BODIES_PER_BLOCK = BLOCK_SIZE * BODIES_PER_THREAD;\n\nstruct Vec3 {\n    double x, y, z;\n    constexpr Vec3(const double x = 0, const double y = 0, const double z = 0) noexcept : x(x), y(y), z(z) {}\n};\n\nstruct Body {\n    Vec3 pos;\n    Vec3 vel;\n};\n\n#define CUDA_CHECK(call)                                                                                    \\\n    do {                                                                                                    \\\n        const cudaError_t err__ = (call);                                                                   \\\n        if (err__ != cudaSuccess) {                                                                         \\\n            fprintf(stderr, \"CUDA error %s at %s:%d: %s\\n\", #call, __FILE__, __LINE__,                      \\\n                    cudaGetErrorString(err__)); the j-loop is walked in ascending order in tiles that\n// are staged through shared memory, which keeps the accumulation order (and\n// therefore the floating-point result) identical to the serial version.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_SIZE) void computeForcesKernel(const double* __restrict__ px,\n                                                                  const double* __restrict__ py,\n                                                                  const double* __restrict__ pz, double* __restrict__ vx,\n                                                                  double* __restrict__ vy, double* __restrict__ vz,\n                                                                  const int n) {\n    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);\n    const int warp = threadIdx.x / WARP_SIZE;\n\n    // Bodies owned by this thread: BODIES_PER_THREAD indices, strided by the\n    // warp width so that every global access stays coalesced.\n    const int base = (blockIdx.x * (BLOCK_SIZE / WARP_SIZE) + warp) * (WARP_SIZE * BODIES_PER_THREAD);\n\n    double xi[BODIES_PER_THREAD], yi[BODIES_PER_THREAD], zi[BODIES_PER_THREAD];\n    double Fx[BODIES_PER_THREAD], Fy[BODIES_PER_THREAD], Fz[BODIES_PER_THREAD];\n\n#pragma unroll\n    for (int u = 0; u < BODIES_PER_THREAD; ++u) {\n        // Out-of-range threads mirror the last body: they compute a throwaway\n        // result but keep the warp converged for the shuffle broadcasts.\n        const int idx = min(base + u * WARP_SIZE + lane, n - 1);\n        xi[u] = px[idx];\n        yi[u] = py[idx];\n        zi[u] = pz[idx];\n        Fx[u] = 0.0;\n        Fy[u] = 0.0;\n        Fz[u] = 0.0;\n    }\n\n    // The j-loop walks 0..n-1 in ascending order in warp-sized tiles that are\n    // broadcast from registers via __shfl_sync, so no shared memory or barriers\n    // are needed and the accumulation order matches the serial version exactly.\n    const int fullTiles = (n / WARP_SIZE) * WARP_SIZE;\n\n    for (int tile = 0; tile < n; tile += WARP_SIZE) {\n        const int rem = min(WARP_SIZE, n - tile);\n        const int jIdx = min(tile + lane, n - 1);\n        const double jx = px[jIdx];\n        const double jy = py[jIdx];\n        const double jz = pz[jIdx];\n\n        const int lim = (tile < fullTiles) ? WARP_SIZE : rem;\n\n#pragma unroll 8\n        for (int k = 0; k < lim; ++k) {\n            const double xj = __shfl_sync(0xffffffffu, jx, k, WARP_SIZE);\n            const double yj = __shfl_sync(0xffffffffu, jy, k, WARP_SIZE);\n            const double zj = __shfl_sync(0xffffffffu, jz, k, WARP_SIZE);\n\n#pragma unroll\n            for (int u = 0; u < BODIES_PER_THREAD; ++u) {\n                const double dx = xj - xi[u];\n                const double dy = yj - yi[u];\n                const double dz = zj - zi[u];\n                const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n                const double invDist = 1.0 / sqrt(distSqr);\n                const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n                Fx[u] += dx * invDist3;\n                Fy[u] += dy * invDist3;\n                Fz[u] += dz * invDist3;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n#pragma unroll\n    for (int u = 0; u < BODIES_PER_THREAD; ++u) {\n        const int idx = base + u * WARP_SIZE + lane;\n        if (idx < n) {\n            vx[idx] += DT * Fx[u];\n            vy[idx] += DT * Fy[u];\n            vz[idx] += DT * Fz[u];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n__global__ void integrateBodiesKernel(double* __restrict__ px, double* __restrict__ py, double* __restrict__ pz,\n                                      const double* __restrict__ vx, const double* __restrict__ vy,\n                                      const double* __restrict__ vz, const int n) {\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (i < n) {\n        px[i] += vx[i] * DT;\n        py[i] += vy[i] * DT;\n        pz[i] += vz[i] * DT;\n    }\n}\n\n// Per-body partial potential energy: body i accumulates the pairs (i, j) with\n// j > i, in ascending j order, exactly as the serial reference does.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_SIZE) void potentialEnergyKernel(const double* __restrict__ px,\n                                                                    const double* __restrict__ py,\n                                                                    const double* __restrict__ pz,\n                                                                    double* __restrict__ partial, const int n) {\n    __shared__ double sx[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double sy[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double sz[BLOCK_SIZE];\n\n    const int tid = threadIdx.x;\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * BLOCK_SIZE + tid;\n    const int iClamped = (i < n) ? i : (n - 1);\n    const double xi = px[iClamped];\n    const double yi = py[iClamped];\n    const double zi = pz[iClamped];\n\n    double e = 0.0;\n\n    // Only tiles that can contain j > i need to be visited.\n    const int firstTile = blockIdx.x * BLOCK_SIZE;\n    for (int tile = firstTile; tile < n; tile += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int rem = min(BLOCK_SIZE, n - tile);\n        if (tid < rem) {\n            sx[tid] = px[tile + tid];\n            sy[tid] = py[tile + tid];\n            sz[tid] = pz[tile + tid];\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        const int kStart = (tile <= i) ? (i - tile + 1) : 0;\n        for (int k = kStart; k < rem; ++k) {\n            const double dx = sx[k] - xi;\n            const double dy = sy[k] - yi;\n            const double dz = sz[k] - zi;\n            const double dist = sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            e -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n\n    if (i < n) {\n        partial[i] = e;\n    }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const DeviceBodies& d, const std::vector<Body>& bodies, double* dPartial) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    if (n == 0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n\n    const int blocks = static_cast<int>((n + BLOCK_SIZE - 1) / BLOCK_SIZE);\n    potentialEnergyKernel<<<blocks, BLOCK_SIZE>>>(d.pos[0], d.pos[1], d.pos[2], dPartial, static_cast<int>(n));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n\n    std::vector<double> terms;\n    terms.reserve(2 * n);\n\n    // Kinetic energy (assuming unit mass)\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        terms.push_back(0.5 * (body.vel.x * body.vel.x +\n                              body.vel.y * body.vel.y +\n                              body.vel.z * body.vel.z));\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies)\n    std::vector<double> partial(n);\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(partial.data(), dPartial, n * sizeof(double), cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost));\n    terms.insert(terms.end(), partial.begin(), partial.end());\n\n    return kahan_sum(terms);\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const std::vector<Body>& bodies) {\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        // Check for NaN or Inf values\n        if (!std::isfinite(body.pos.x) || !std::isfinite(body.pos.y) || !std::isfinite(body.pos.z) ||\n            !std::isfinite(body.vel.x) || !std::isfinite(body.vel.y) || !std::isfinite(body.vel.z)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value in body state\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        // Check for extreme values (bodies shouldn't fly off to infinity)\n        const double maxPos = 1e6;\n        const double maxVel = 1e6;\n        if (std::abs(body.pos.x) > maxPos || std::abs(body.pos.y) > maxPos || std::abs(body.pos.z) > maxPos) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body position exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(body.vel.x) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.y) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.z) > maxVel) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of bodies (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -s <num>     Number of simulation steps (default: {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeoY7ezris1d4WtGTVfxq","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Fresh configure + build is clean and warning-free. Done.\n\n## What I did\n\n**`nbody/nbody.cpp`** — CUDA port, unconditionally GPU-parallel:\n\n- **SoA device layout** (`px,py,pz,vx,vy,vz`) so every global access is coalesced; the host-side `Body` AoS is kept only for I/O and validation.\n- **`computeForcesKernel`**: one thread per body *i*. The *j*-loop is traversed in warp-sized tiles — each lane loads one *j*-position and the tile is replayed via `__shfl_sync` broadcasts, so no shared memory and no barriers are needed. Ragged tails are handled by clamping indices (out-of-range threads stay converged and just don't write back).\n- **`integrateBodiesKernel`** kept as a separate kernel: positions must not be updated while other blocks still read them.\n- **Whole step loop runs on the GPU** — no per-step transfers. One H2D at the start, one D2H at the end, both through pinned memory and both inside the timed region. Context creation and allocation happen before the timer.\n- **`computeTotalEnergy`** (the `-v` path) was also O(n²) and would have dominated runtime on the host; it now runs as a triangular GPU kernel producing per-body partials, Kahan-summed on the host.\n\n**`nbody/CMakeLists.txt`** — compiles `nbody.cpp` as CUDA via `set_source_files_properties(... LANGUAGE CUDA)`, `CUDA_ARCHITECTURES native`, `-O3` with host flags passed through `-Xcompiler`. Executable name unchanged; no new files or dependencies.\n\n## Correctness\n\nThe tiling preserves the exact summation order of the serial code (ascending *j*, per-*i* accumulator). I verified this directly: built with `-fmad=false` against the original built with `-ffp-contract=off`, the FNV hashes are **bit-identical** for n = 1, 2, 7, 33, 1025, 3000.\n\nThe shipped build leaves FMA contraction on (it's faster), so results differ from the gcc build in the last few ulps — ~1e-12 relative after 10 steps of a chaotic system. That gap is compiler-inherent, not an artifact of parallelization: the original gcc `-O3 -march=native` build and a gcc `-ffp-contract=off` build already disagree with each other by the same amount. Edge cases (n=0/1/2, non-multiples of 32, `-h`, bad flags) all behave as before.\n\n## Performance (RTX 3090, single GPU, 10 steps)\n\n| N | CPU | GPU | Speedup |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1024 | 77 ms | 6 ms | 13× |\n| 4096 | 1235 ms | 27 ms | 46× |\n| 10000 | 7381 ms | 106 ms | 70× |\n| 30000 | 66688 ms | 846 ms | 79× |\n| 100000 | — | 9117 ms | — |\n\nScaling is cleanly quadratic from 50k up (2.3 s → 9.1 s → 36.5 s), i.e. the GPU is saturated. Two tuning notes from measurement: 32-thread blocks beat 128/256 by ~1.3× even at large N (finer block granularity avoids wave quantization across 82 SMs, and 4× at N=1024), and register blocking (2–4 bodies/thread) *hurt* — the kernel is bound by the FP64 `sqrt`+divide pipeline, not by memory.\n\nOne option I deliberately left on the table: swapping `1.0/sqrt(x)` for the intrinsic `rsqrt(x)` measured **1.44× faster** (1110 → 772 ms at N=30000). I did not take it, because `rsqrt` carries 1 ulp of error against the correctly-rounded divide-and-sqrt, which is a deliberate accuracy reduction rather than a compiler-level difference. Say the word if you'd prefer the speed."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":302,"cache_read_input_tokens":73218,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":302},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8002a813-d653-45fe-a264-6f3936259a5b","uuid":"c20ef021-548c-4373-938b-d2c99b781f01","timestamp":"2026-09-07T05:36:38.198Z","request_id":"req_011CeoY7eETpdJjj1N2HcH2z"}
